### Multi-drag transform-gizmo sessions

Move, Rotate and Scale now keep a desktop-style session across released drags. Each accepted nonidentity drag bakes from current geometry and rebases the idle preview to that result. Check keeps the geometry and exits; floating Undo moves one native session history entry, stopping at its original scene. Generic Undo/Redo retains the desktop fallback at that boundary. Cancel restores the original shared scene, mask and content identity as one undoable restoration, including after history trimming. Header Reset cancels the gizmo before the existing broader reset.

Each physical press captures its selected/current-view target before queued work. Preview/release waits for that target acknowledgement; an empty target never expands to the whole scene. Identity releases create no geometry history. The three-button tray uses the desktop 90 by 28 layout, exact localized Check/Undo/Cancel titles, event-driven placement and existing input ownership. Captured Ctrl/Cmd+Z cancels unfinished input before earlier history. Enter/Escape finish/cancel, Basic/Pro preserves the idle transform gizmo, and accepted geometry publishes with its identity preview before old GPU retirement.

`proTransformSession:2` requires exact gizmo session metadata and independent request, receipt, read-only and recovery proofs. The native original Cancel baseline is retained lazily without copying Gaussian payloads merely to arm the tool. Numeric transform behavior stays absolute with explicit Apply/Cancel. Codec3 finalizes gizmo owners on fresh worker restoration while retaining accepted scene/history; same-worker graphics recovery preserves the session and its Cancel baseline. ABI24, camera ABI4, protocol28, UI23, selectionActions2, Range1, pivot2 and receipt2 remain stable. [Source references and adaptations](docs/third-party/GIZMO_SESSION_ACTIONS_REFERENCES.md).

Validation passed: 6,139 CPU tests in 187 files, 113 production-browser cases in one complete run, 14 executed native module suites, and 90 Python checks (1 existing host-dependent probe skipped). [Validation record](docs/validation/runs/gizmo-session-actions-web-20261017-01/validation.json).

### Continuous manual selection history

Manual floating controls now follow the desktop's continuous selection session. Switching manual tools, rearming the current tool, changing Basic/Pro, and graphics-only recovery preserve the entry boundary. Leaving selection mode and re-entering starts a fresh boundary. A zero-selection tray remains visible only for unfinished work or a qualifying selection edit at the top of Undo. Content-changing top entries and history from before entry cannot keep an empty tray visible.

The native core publishes required `undoSelectionRevision`: the creation or latest accepted amendment revision of the top selection Undo entry, or zero for other entries. Undo/Redo, copies, transactions and history trimming preserve the original stamps; no-ops do not refresh them. Restored history receives zero runtime stamps, while its ordinary revision and Undo/Redo remain intact. Client/worker checks independently bind this scalar across read-only calls, previews, operation outcomes and recovery. Waiting manual Undo requires both the acknowledged recorded owner and qualifying session history.

`selectionActions:2` adds one uint64 per native history Revision, with actual deque allocation accounted through the existing native allocator. There is no additional per-splat cache, export field or durable codec change. Core ABI24, camera ABI4, protocol28, UI23, filter2, Range1, pivot2, receipt2 and codec3 remain stable. [Source references and adaptations](docs/third-party/SELECTION_HISTORY_REFERENCES.md).

### Floating selection confirmation actions

Manual selection now has the desktop Check, Undo and X controls. Check joins released clicks and tolerance amendments, verifies native owner retirement, and returns to Orbit with the selection intact. Undo prioritizes unfinished input and the latest queued release before completed history. X cancels unfinished work, joins cleanup and color materialization, clears highlights, and keeps the tool armed. C and Ctrl/Cmd+Z use these same admitted actions. Source guards and a joined action fence prevent stale completion and overlapping edits.

Filter Undo discards a changed or in-flight preview; an unchanged preview permits committed history Undo. X records the last acknowledged visible preview before clearing it, retaining the enclosure, Combine and even invalid dirty desired parameters in a paused filter. Native `filter.changed` detects same-count membership changes. The explicit `visiblePreview:true` confirmation can preserve a visible epoch after newer work was canceled. `toleranceRecorded` distinguishes a real Color/Fill click Undo from a no-op or an amendment back to baseline. Identical acknowledged tolerance requests skip redundant native work; refused requests remain retryable and raw Radius preferences remain intact.

The shared three-button tray is 90 by 28 pixels at normal scale, uses event-driven release-safe placement and obstacle/capture/popup suppression, and retains its anchor across paused filter restarts. Floating Undo has a distinct accessible name while keeping the desktop icon and tooltip. Duplicate overlay reconciliation found by the production regression was fixed with distinct source-bound component keys.

`selectionFilter:2` and `selectionActions:1` retain core ABI24, protocol28, UI23, camera ABI4, Range1, pivot2, receipt2 and codec3. No additional per-splat cache or durable history field was added. [Source references and adaptations](docs/third-party/FLOATING_SELECTION_ACTIONS_REFERENCES.md).

### Color/Fill Range and logarithmic Radius

Color and Fill now have independent viewer-session Range and Radius preferences in Basic and Pro. A pick freezes the raw native Color contributor center or the press-time Fill depth-surface center. Sphere membership is applied before Combine against the original click checkpoint, preserving Add/Remove selections outside the sphere and the separate captured current-view rule. Amendments keep one Undo; metadata-only changes preserve selection notifications and history. Legacy omitted-Range calls retain their prior ignored-input behavior, while explicit Range requests reject replacement of frozen inputs.

Radius uses the existing bounded sampled scene statistics, half the morphology maximum and a fresh geometric-mean default. Raw typed preferences survive scene/profile changes; only effective operands are clamped and quantized. Pointer/key/numeric edits settle before native selection work; the local Tools-only sphere has 192 source segments and follows Combine colors. Continuous next-pick hover sampling remains omitted, and Fill remains synchronous within the existing limited-scene scope.

The additive `colorFillRange:1` capability retains ABI24, protocol28, UI23, camera ABI4, receipt2 and codec3. Worker/client/controller checks authenticate Range independently across request, commit and rollback. Readonly statistics and pivot focus preserve compatible completed owners. Recovery retains accepted selection/history and viewer preferences, while a new click reacquires its transient map and center. ### Automatic selection pivots and derived refresh

Completed selection membership changes now choose Selection when nonempty; clearing a Selection pivot chooses Origin. Automatic refresh waits for physical selection, released clicks, tolerance/map work and active numeric editing to settle. Tolerance slider adjustment defers refresh during pointer capture or held adjustment keys; a focused idle slider permits refresh. Scene/Selection references refresh when their geometry changes, while geometry-only changes preserve Custom and Origin choices. Retained transforms keep the reference in their original base coordinates; retiring a recipe resets its parameters, carries Custom through the accepted matrix once and refreshes derived references against the accepted scene.

The native `selectionCommitRevision` notification compares selected bits and mask cardinality, including different members with the same selected count. Draft previews/cancellation and content bakes with unchanged membership do not advance it. It belongs to the worker lifetime: transaction rollback restores the preceding value, and fresh-worker recovery starts at zero without inventing a selection event. `transformPivot: 2` requires and validates it. Automatic focus queries retain settled Color/Fill, grade and opacity owners, with the same owner guard at admission and every cooperative step. Successful graphics recovery and canceled source-load cleanup clear a remembered query refusal. No scene-format, checkpoint-codec, receipt or camera version changes are introduced by this phase.

[Pivot references](docs/third-party/PIVOT_REFERENCES.md) identify the desktop behavior and browser adaptation. Validation passed: 5,837 CPU tests, 98 distinct production-browser cases across the full run and targeted rerun, and 11 native module suites; 90 Python verification checks passed and one host-dependent filesystem probe was skipped. [Validation record](docs/validation/runs/selection-pivot-web-20261016-01/validation.json).

The implementation goal remains active. A source-backed Color/Fill Range and logarithmic Radius design is ready for implementation, including independent preferences, same-pick amendments and sphere feedback. Dedicated Mac hardware testing remains optional.


### Explicit Pro pivot controls

Pro now cycles the pivot through Scene, Selection and Origin, skipping Selection when empty; a manually entered Pivot becomes Custom and its next cycle returns to Scene. The control uses the committed SplatView 1.0.2 icons and exact label/tooltips across eight locales. Selection focus uses the native covariance-diagonal three-sigma AABB midpoint. A bound readonly query reads canonical selection in the retained transform's original coordinates, independent of its current-view target mask. Its admitted cooperative owner is at most 512 bytes; the existing synchronous focus calculation during `beginTransform` remains unchanged.

A neutral pivot-reference change preserves translation and other pending fields without baking content or adding Undo. A reference change affecting a nonneutral transform stays in the explicit preview/Apply workflow. Gizmos use the chosen reference; Reset selects Selection when nonempty and Origin otherwise; source replacement starts at Scene. Canceled or stale focus completion cannot change a successor document. [Pivot references](docs/third-party/PIVOT_REFERENCES.md) bind the source inputs and browser adaptations. Validation passed: 5,774 CPU tests, 96 production-browser tests, 10 native module suites and 47 Python verification tests. [Validation record](docs/validation/runs/pivot-controls-web-20261016-01/validation.json).

The implementation goal remains active. Automatic pivot default/refresh after completed selection membership changes remains open, separately from the explicit cycle. Color/Fill Range and Radius, joined floating Check/Undo/X actions, current tutorial qualification, complete visual/assistive equivalence, retained-format/recovery and deployed HTTPS work, and measured performance/capacity remain open. The one-million loaded preview policy is not a measured operating guarantee. Device hardware testing remains optional.


### Functional Pro Duplicate

Pro Duplicate is functional through the native command, worker, controller and GPU publication path. It copies complete 144-byte Gaussian records in source order, preserves deleted masks, deselects originals and selects the appended copies. Current-view scope is captured once. One history step restores the complete scene, selection and frame metadata through Undo/Redo; a successful Duplicate ends the prior alignment Reset baseline, and the next alignment starts on the grown scene. The immutable original-source/subset binding stays unchanged.

The Pro action uses the committed desktop Duplicate icon and exact label/three-line tooltip in English and seven translated locales. Unchanged copies use the desktop green `(0.49, 0.824, 0.573)` at a `0.72` mix, through presentation uniforms; exported and captured source colors remain unchanged. Editing the copies clears their green marker and permits another Duplicate. Native records, history, duplicate lineage and admitted GPU buffers publish together, with rollback/recovery retaining the accepted state on refusal or interruption.

Wire protocol 28, UI-worker contract 23 and core ABI 24 advertise Duplicate 1, transaction receipt 2 and recovery checkpoint 2; camera ABI 4 remains unchanged. Codec 3 authenticates the grown or shrunk history graph, its current counts and per-scene duplicate range against the immutable import binding. The retained `N + K <= 1,000,000` loaded-splat cap includes deleted records, and old-plus-candidate native/GPU overlap must fit admission. This policy cap is not measured one-million-splat operating capacity.

The [functional Duplicate validation](docs/validation/runs/pro-duplicate-web-20261016-01/validation.json) records fresh native/web builds, typechecks, five native contract suites, 30 build/provenance tool tests, 5681 CPU cases and 94 production browser cases. [Source references](docs/third-party/DUPLICATE_REFERENCES.md) identify the exact desktop inputs and browser integration. Earlier Duplicate foundation/open-work notes below retain their historical scope.

The implementation goal remains active. Pivot-mode cycling, Color/Fill Range and Radius, joined floating Check/Undo/X actions, current tutorial qualification, full visual/assistive equivalence and measured operating capacity remain open, along with the other retained format/recovery and HTTPS requirements. Available-browser qualification is the baseline; device hardware testing is optional.

# SplatView web implementation status

### Desktop navigation return and repeated lifecycle observations; implementation in progress

Orbit/Look now follows the frozen desktop's return rule for manual selection tools: pressing the navigation control exits an active selection tool to Orbit, while cancelling an armed alignment action still enters Look. Cancelling an unfinished Polygon restores its selection baseline. Two actual-App Basic/Pro cases cover all seven manual tools, alignment cancellation, executable Redo and unchanged full-SH3 scene export. The existing worker, native editing operations and browser navigation policy remain authoritative.

The new bounded lifecycle collector observes 20 complete cycles in one production page, browser context and retained controller bridge, without reload or bridge replacement. Every cycle opens the pinned 256,000-record SH0 scene, selects and deletes the independently declared anchor, executes Undo/Redo, exports all visible records to SPLAT with explicit acknowledgement, downloads and independently decodes a JPEG capture, replaces the source and joins scene Close. Complete native mask artifacts, export membership and named pending-owner barriers accompany the raw records. This is a specific repeated lifecycle observation, not a general operating budget or measured scene-capacity guarantee.

[Current bounded validation](docs/validation/runs/lifecycle-20261015-01/validation.json) binds 8 passing production browser cases across 2 files, 10 passing development browser cases across 1 files and 151 focused CPU cases across 6 files. The qualification records the actual reports, source preimages and independently declared inventories; unit fixtures qualify collector consistency rules, not real performance. The camera screenshot helper excludes the canvas focus outline and separate brush-size hint DOM feedback from scene-pixel comparison. Polygon assertions check that active polygon geometry is cleared while permitting the intended hover cursor; scene-pixel, native-state and history checks remain intact. Product styling is unchanged, and the three earlier raw camera diagnostics, two actual camera probes and executed test preimages remain preserved. The raw retention report retains one page clock, logical-ledger observations, currently addressable native accounts and actual joined cleanup. These measurements do not represent physical JavaScript, process or GPU memory, and no latency, sustained-performance, full-matrix or capacity acceptance is claimed.

Generic non-SPZ4 export still has a known native-workspace and Blob ownership accounting gap. A zero logical ledger after joined Close does not prove that every export allocation was represented during its lifetime. Complete generic export accounting remains open; the separate explicit SPZ4 path and existing admission limits keep their original scope. This packet does not change arbitrary scene caps or close that gap through the repeated test alone.

The production build and both TypeScript checks pass. Shipping contains 101 files (97 runtime artifacts, 3 maps and its manifest), with zero stale assets. Cleanup removed 16 regenerable cache files (2,798,323 bytes), preserving source, fixtures, raw observations and reports. Owned local services and browser contexts have stopped and joined. No commit, push or deployment was performed.

The implementation goal remains active. Broader desktop appearance and assistive comparisons, remaining image/format/recovery behavior, deployed HTTPS, complete generic export ownership and measured limited-size operating budgets remain in the plan. Mac hardware is optional. Full appearance, release readiness, performance and capacity remain unaccepted.

### Sealed historical Deferred scene input and popup ownership; bounded local qualification

Scene selection presses admitted during genuine color processing now retain an ordered physical input stream instead of disappearing behind the busy state. The browser bounds it to 32 unreplayed presses and 65,536 event records, preserves every accepted motion record, and yields after each 512 replayed records. A press snapshots Combine, selection options, Brush/Volume radius and Color/Fill tolerance. Replay joins the original grading operation and color-session settlement, then applies accepted work in FIFO order against successive committed baselines, preserving native Undo/Redo ownership.

The owner is bound to its source/document generation, camera, renderer, dimensions and viewport bounds. Focus transfer, capture loss, cancellation, hiding, popups, source replacement and invalidating work retire it. Expected release of an earlier physical capture does not cancel a newer held press. Native mouse-down click counts remain available to Polygon, and a healthy live Single rollback can restore its baseline without dropping its physical reentry or later accepted records. Explicit Apply and explicit Auto remain the browser grading workflow; later grading changes are fenced while deferred input owns the scene. This does not add the desktop's separate 32-color-action queue or change the existing independent 1,024-request released Color/Fill FIFO.

Selection and Auto/Presets portals now retain outside-press ownership through primary click, physical right contextmenu and middle/right auxclick, including both sibling events when one right press emits both. A physical contextmenu with detail zero remains owned; keyboard clicks, ContextMenu and Shift+F10 retain independent activation. Fresh presses and lifecycle retirement release the previous owner. The actual underlying Tolerance editor, native/GPU state and executable Redo have independent browser oracles. Behavior and bounded browser adaptations are documented in the [deferred input source references](docs/third-party/DEFERRED_INPUT_REFERENCES.md).

These observed counts overlap and remain separate. The qualifying browser cohorts have zero failures, skips, retries or flakes. Development browser coverage is a set of named subsets; no complete development-suite rerun is claimed. Native artifacts and ABI16/protocol22/UI-worker18/camera ABI4 remain unchanged; no native rebuild or standalone C++ test rerun was performed.

### Sealed historical W18 opacity Fade / boost; bounded local qualification

Current contract: core ABI16/protocol22/UI-worker18/`absoluteOpacitySession1`/`opacityBaseStream1`, camera ABI4 unchanged. Pro Color adds **Fade / boost** after Tint, using the captured [processing_ui.cpp:248](reference/targets/windows-r0-20261012-02/source/src/processing_ui.cpp) control: 0-500%, neutral 100%, separate from grading Fade. Slider, numeric percentage entry and keyboard edits stage a cheap GPU preview; explicit Apply opacity accepts it, and pending Apply requests coalesce to the latest desired value. No scene-sized CPU bake runs per slider event.

Native begin/apply/reset/end uses an absolute fixed entry scene/mask/scope and retained entry GPU payload. Repeated Apply derives from entry rather than the previously accepted alpha; zero and saturation need no inverse reconstruction. Changed accepted Apply creates normal Undo history; accepted no-op creates none and preserves Redo. Reset restores entry and is undoable when changed. Cancel opacity preview restores the last accepted scale/controls and cancels pending work without undoing earlier accepted Apply. Clean end materializes accepted content before releasing the GPU base.

Captured [processing.cpp:957](reference/targets/windows-r0-20261012-02/source/src/processing.cpp) freezes selected/global membership and excludes deleted records; [processing.cpp:984](reference/targets/windows-r0-20261012-02/source/src/processing.cpp) defines scale `s` in 0-5 and `clamp(s <= 1 ? alpha*s : alpha + (1-alpha)*(s-1)/2, 0, 1)`. Boost revives alpha-zero records and saturates safely. The native adapter changes opacity only, preserving XYZ, covariance, directional SH, bounds, alignment, default view, masks and source identity. The existing 320-byte shared frame carries scale at float 59 and independent opacity scope at uint 79. Display projection and the downsampled map projection apply alpha before culling/support, then blending, contributor picks, depth/opacity, raw RGB weights and full-frame visibility consume the same preview. Alpha edits invalidate pending renderer queries and retained selection-map identities synchronously, including selected-mask dependency only for active selected scope. RGB grading/highlight remain excluded from raw map identity. The stable depth order already contains transparent sources, so opacity-only changes refresh projection without another depth-sort dispatch or scene-sized CPU alpha buffer.

[Canonical bounded receipt](docs/validation/runs/opacity-20261015-01/validation-01.json) qualifies local Windows Chromium/WebGPU: [CPU](test-results-opacity-units-03.json) **3,318/101**; [focus](test-results-opacity-focus-02.json) **18/2**; [full development](test-results-opacity-full-01.json) **903/48**; [production](test-results-opacity-production-01.json) **84/3** cases/files, plus [actual native execution](docs/validation/runs/opacity-20261015-01/native-execution-01.json) of all 21 linked mains. ### Selected collection navigation; bounded local qualification

This section records the sealed pre-SPZ collection predecessor. At that seal, the successor implemented **Open folder**, real relative-path folder selection, directory drop and ordinary multiple-file catalogs at **core ABI13/protocol18/UI-worker14/groupedSog3**, camera ABI4 unchanged. [Synchronous drop capture](src/io/collectionAcquisition.ts) retains explicit entry authority before any await; [immutable owned catalogs](src/io/sceneCollection.ts) retain lazy file/directory paths. Only eligible primaries become scene entries. No metadata or resource body is read until its scene activates, no WebP resource is treated as an ordinary scene, and browser selection gives no implicit filesystem-neighbor access.

Desktop edge chevrons and PageUp/PageDown provide nonwrapping navigation without a sidebar. Tools hides arrows but does not exclude scene-focused Page keys; collisions, chrome fading, repeat/modifiers/composition, text/popups/pointer ownership and load/save/processing barriers are respected. Existing Save/Discard/Stay and matching full-scene output acknowledgement protect dirty documents. Candidate CPU/GPU publication swaps catalog/index with document/key/camera before retiring old owners. Failed/cancelled next preserves the accepted index, exact document, edits, key and executable Redo. Cancel/supersession/Close/dispose joins real scan/getFile and source/GPU lifetimes before catalog credit is released; stale delivery cannot start or publish a newer load.

This is an **explicit browser extension** over native immediate-sibling navigation: nested groups and ordinary scenes may be discovered throughout the selected collection. The pinned COLMAPView [scanner](https://github.com/ColmapView/Colmapview.github.io/blob/0a1d01c3c8c65f9c0a244f4608c50558c221c5fd/src/utils/fileScanning.ts) and [drop payload](https://github.com/ColmapView/Colmapview.github.io/blob/0a1d01c3c8c65f9c0a244f4608c50558c221c5fd/src/hooks/fileDropzoneDropPayload.ts), and the [Entries API](https://wicg.github.io/entries-api/), are behavior/API references. COLMAPView is AGPL-3.0; no copied or adapted code is used in the independent collection adapter. Desktop command meanings and UI/core alignment remain authoritative.

Discovery limits are **65,536 entries including directories**, **4,194,304 UTF-16 path units**, **65,536 components** and **depth 256**. Each activated scene retains the **256 MiB unique-original cap**, one-million loaded preview and separate metadata/decoded/native/GPU/device/old-plus-candidate admission. These bounds establish no maximum-capacity/performance or physical-memory claim. The [12-leaf nested fixture](tests/fixtures/scene-collection-nested-v1/manifest.json) carries actual uppercase `asset/META.JSON`, nested textures and `../shared` resources. An [independent eight-original key](tests/fixtures/scene-collection-nested-v1/expected-source-key.json) includes unused declarations while omitting private neighbors and outer-root labels; The independent eight-original digest is `dabb154326d88c7b1ae2b12e346643a0492afe55c8740f57850800e73542ef35`. v2-SH3 numeric/WebP goldens and the 182-leaf higher-SH fixture packet remain unchanged.

### Historical absolute grading and Auto Color; bounded local qualification

The following sealed packet preserves its original counts, identities and scope. References to current state in this historical packet describe its captured Auto Color snapshot; Center supersedes the live ABI and app identity.

The October 13 increment implements all 15 grading fields against one immutable native session base, with Auto, Exposure, Contrast and White balance in Basic and Pro. It introduces core ABI8/protocol12/UI-worker8, retaining camera ABI1, and the coherent capabilities `absoluteColorSession:1`, `autoColor:1` and `colorBaseStream:1`. Browser menus use the seven Basic and 40 Pro presets. The increment has bounded local acceptance below; whole W18 interaction/visual parity remains open.

Automatic analysis reads the captured base scene's source DC coefficients, excludes deleted splats and uses the captured selected/global scope. At most 65,536 deterministic samples feed exact desktop percentiles and trimmed statistics. The four suggestions update only their owned brightness, contrast, temperature and tint fields; the other grade fields survive. This source analysis is separate from the rendered-view histogram, which includes background and highlights. Manual equality with the applied grade is a true no-op; explicit Auto may create a new history revision even when its nonneutral output quantizes to the same records. Reset previews the original base; applying neutral or settling that preview after a prior bake restores native data. A color session can contain several Undo items.

The native document holds the bake while the active GPU scene retains the original base and draws the absolute grade once. Settled active-session native export reads baked document data without changing the base, metadata or history; pending export is refused and is not an operation-preserving observer. A typed base stream permits recovery of the retained display owner. Source/session/base/epoch identity checks and monotonic safe-integer IDs protect acknowledgements and recovery.

Color commands stage native edits and metadata before atomic publication. The controller owns a transaction through admission, the GPU completion fence and commit; a failed bake/materialization joins rollback before retiring its owners. Selection, mask, transform, history and source boundaries first settle grading and materialize the baked GPU scene; recovery streams the base when the native session remains active. Native cancellation retires analysis/bake scratch and restores applied metadata; controller rollback can preserve the user's entry or newer manual preview separately. Actual grants and census observations remain separate from conservative controller allowances; no closed heap/codec/filesystem quota or million-splat browser-performance guarantee follows. The 256 MiB/one-million-preview limits and browser-only delivery scope are unchanged.

Retained preview/Apply/Undo/export traces and deferred pointer-press replay while busy remain open. Cheap preset hover and exact automatic slider commit cadence are conditional enhancements; Auto-on-hover analysis is excluded from required scope. Matched desktop/web visual acceptance, deployed-host/offline qualification and broader recovery remain separate work. The [pinned MIT PlayCanvas review](docs/third-party/PLAYCANVAS_REFERENCES.md) is reference-only: no reviewed PlayCanvas code was adapted into this increment, and desktop SplatView remains the grading/Auto authority.

The stable native owner retains scene/mask without copying geometry, owns six admitted POD arrays and publishes sorted sparse overrides through one existing `SceneEdits::commit`. Cancellation, rollback, replacement and completion retire scratch and the empty global pointer's deleter-held Work service. The `MessageChannel` worker pump runs 4,096-work steps in batches capped at 8 ms or 256 steps with a coarse/frozen clock, checking cancellation between steps and task yields. Final sparse conversion and native COW/history publication remain synchronous O(N+K); the batch target is not a wall-clock guarantee.

Executed WASM32 layout measures the full owner at 6,168 bytes, distinct from its 6,024-byte `Job`. Scratch is `S = 37*N + 44*M` bytes, or 42,767,124 bytes at one million splats/131,071 nodes. Controller policy reserves `S + 6168 + 24*N + 4 MiB`, plus existing `historyBytes` separately for transaction-copy overlap. Native grants/census and publication/staging retain their own ownership. This conservative operation allowance is not a closed heap/codec/filesystem quota or a million-splat browser performance claim. Limits remain 256 MiB per scene and one million loaded preview splats.

### Rendered-view histogram and scene statistics

The histogram/stats implementation has bounded local qualification. Its desktop contract is captured `src/app_ui.cpp`, lines 1012–1142, `src/renderer.cpp`, line 1900, and `src/shaders/histogram.hlsl`. It samples actual display RGB, including background, grading, selection highlights and deleted-source removal; it does not use clean `capture()` pixels or source DC coefficients. Statistics report selected/visible splats and `SH{min(degree,3)}`, where visible means loaded minus deleted, not a frustum/occlusion count.

The pinned field is 54 pixels tall, with a 4-pixel plot inset and three 256-bin lines sharing a `log1p` peak. Colors are `#ec6464`/`#7dd292`/`#69a5f5` at opacity 0.82, with no axes or legend. WebGPU samples the real current canvas texture with `TEXTURE_BINDING` on a center grid of `min(width,256) × min(height,144)` pixels, rounds clamped RGB to byte bins, and reads back only 3,072 bytes asynchronously. Sampling runs at most 5 Hz on changed views, with no idle loop. The optional sampler latches failure for that renderer rather than retrying continuously.

A separate 12,304-byte reservation covers GPU/CPU overlap and grants no native admission credit. Hide, Close, replacement and recovery invalidate stale results before publication. Native/WASM, census, 256 MiB input and one-million-preview policy stay unchanged. Earlier Settings and viewer-shell receipts remain bound to their own exact packet identities.

### Bounded scene-size delivery scope

The user accepts limited file sizes and asked to follow COLMAPView. The reference uses format-specific checks and large-file warnings rather than a blanket desktop input cap. Keep SplatView's existing **256 MiB (268,435,456 bytes) per-scene input** and **one million loaded preview splats**; central enforcement before body reads, candidate creation/staging and worker decode is implemented across controller, probe, client and worker, with focused local qualification below. This is SplatView's own policy, not a copied COLMAPView hard cap. Future unpacked/grouped scenes must sum unique original metadata and all final declared resources under the same input cap, retain per-resource bounds and load only the selected candidate.

The smaller product scope lets desktop viewer/UI and feasible Basic/Pro features proceed without a general raw allocator quota project as a prerequisite. Preserve existing checked expansion, decoded/header/texture/count, GPU/device and old-plus-candidate admission limits: compressed bytes alone do not bound decoded memory. Candidate failure must retain the old edited scene, view/key, output and executable Redo. General C++/codec/filesystem quota infrastructure is deferred unless a concrete supported path needs it. The observational census, JSON fallback and raw C/MEMFS exclusions remain unchanged. Grouped loading and complete desktop parity are still open; historical receipts retain their exact identities, limits and scope.

The shared [scene input policy](src/document/sceneInputLimits.ts) reads intrinsic Blob size through the platform getter, so a caller's shadowed `size` property cannot bypass the check. The controller checks initial choice and adjacent navigation before dirty-document decisions or candidate work; the probe checks before its bounded header read; CoreClient checks before request IDs, admission hooks, reads or messages; the already initialized worker independently checks the validated descriptor before source progress, staging, source-specific native allocation/decoding or range I/O. The empty viewer and Open button show “Up to 256 MiB per file”; oversized sources receive the same explicit limit diagnostic. Exactly 256 MiB remains eligible for further format/device/admission checks, with no promise that every such file opens.

Its remote automatic-download thresholds are warnings/confirmation policy, its archive bound is separate, and its SOG/GPU limits are format/device checks. This selected local observation is not a complete repository capture or proof of deployed website bytes. The user's authorization to reuse COLMAPView baseline/logic stands; desktop SplatView remains the core/UI authority, and direct reuse still records file/revision/license provenance.

### Bounded SOG JSON cleanup authority

The actual SOG loader now preflights the exact borrowed `meta.json` bytes with a fixed-array, nonrecursive UTF-8/JSON grammar scanner. It qualifies at most 16 MiB, depth32, 65,536 tokens, 1,048,576 decoded UTF-16 units per string, 4,194,304 string units in total and 128 characters per numeric lexeme, with finite binary64 range checking. Duplicate keys and overwritten values count toward qualification. These bounds select a cleanup route; they add no input rejection, command limit, quota or admission credit.

A private, noncopyable `ParsedSogMetadata` owns qualification, destruction authority and the parsed DOM in that declaration order, keeping authority alive through normal destruction and parse exceptions. Qualified destruction avoids nlohmann's allocating flatten stack. An unqualified nested owner explicitly masks outer authority and restores it on retirement; it retains the original parser/destructor. The disposable overlay generates one pinned nlohmann header for the core and linked JSON contracts. Frozen dependencies stay unchanged, and the generator and generated inputs participate in build verification.

Source content keys also format the complete 32-byte SHA-256 digest into a fixed 64-character lowercase hexadecimal buffer before constructing one owning string. This replaces the vendor stream formatter, whose allocation failure can be swallowed and return a partial digest. The single final string allocation now propagates failure before candidate publication; hashing and cancellation behavior stay aligned with the desktop source. Independent Python `hashlib` archive hashes provide the contract oracle.

[Fresh linked native execution02](docs/validation/runs/native-json-cleanup-20261012-01/native-contracts.json) passes all eight mains. The JSON suite reports 13 cases/3,838 assertions, 68 scans, 46 qualification refusals, six qualified destructions, 98 parse and 247 candidate allocation refusals, six ordinary refusals, five accepted fallback variants and the retained 65,536-point command path. It observes zero C++ allocation attempts/backend events after injected refusal through the observed unwind boundaries and a maximum sampled destructor stack delta of 1,056 bytes; independent archive identity checks total four.

### SPZ stream and transactional export cleanup

The browser overlay now closes initialized legacy-gzip SPZ inflate/deflate streams on vector exceptions and ordinary codec errors. The frozen desktop source stays unchanged, and the transformation helper participates in the native build identity. Export serializes into privately owned temporary files, checks the complete file read and stages replacement bytes. It prepares the typed reply and retires the parsed request and file owners before swapping output; failures preserve the previous output, edited scene and undo/redo state. Old and new output coexist until publication, so this increment adds no memory quota or capacity guarantee.

### W13 portable-view bounded local acceptance

The [portable01 receipt](docs/validation/portable-view-local.json) accepts its captured bounded local source. The [portable container](src/io/portableSavedView.ts), [exact-ID pair retrieval](src/io/savedViewStorage.ts), controller import/export and Settings controls are implemented. The two-member stored ZIP contains only `view.json` and `thumbnail.png`; bounded headers precede member reads, CRC/SHA/PNG validation precedes storage, and a complete source fingerprint plus decoded kind must match. Import assigns a fresh local ID and timestamps, commits one atomic pair, then restores only the still-current document at its current viewport aspect. Export uses the immutable stored pair and retains its output until object-URL revocation; Close/dispose join pending transfers and retire downloads. A canceled export separately owns its original comparison record while a newer view can replace the controller record. Neither action clears dirty scene state or creates a scene-output receipt.

<a id="w13-functional-timing-boundary"></a>
**Functional timing boundary:** in full01, adding the reported wall-clock start to the independently measured monotonic duration produces an end 982.901 ms after the observed process finish. The receipt binds installed Playwright 1.64.0's separate clock sources and preserves the raw values; the cause of this offset is unproven. Functional acceptance checks the report and every actual test's wall-clock start within the observed process, finite nonnegative durations, exact case inventory, successful terminal and report/output/artifact bindings. It does not accept aggregate end containment or performance. Mac frame times, latency and operating budgets require the separate benchmark and hardware gates.

The written client keeps File/Blob acquisition on the main thread and sends validated source descriptors plus sequential ranges of at most 1 MiB to the verified worker. Receive credit precedes each request; transfer and native consumption have separate acknowledgements. `stageSingleSourceInput()` copies each range into one admitted final native input and hashes those same bytes before consumption, then hashes the bounded canonical encoding. The worker decodes that allocation and returns a complete fingerprint using the actual decoded format. Synchronous native decode cannot process cooperative Cancel messages during the call. Main-thread cancellation terminates the independent candidate worker and still joins pending Blob reads before reservations become reusable.

The controller now installs the validated fingerprint, frozen catalog/index, client, scene and camera in the same synchronous winner publication before old retirement. A candidate native-module baseline remains admitted through canceled-read settlement; a winning module's baseline remains admitted for its active scene and old-scene retirement. The scene-free startup client is reused for the first candidate rather than retained as an extra idle module. Disposal clears fingerprint/catalog/index/deferred navigation immediately and releases scene/module reservations after retirement. These source lifetime assertions pass within source04 local regression; retained-heap accounting and actual platform/memory qualification remain separate.

Its units include prepared encoder58, read broker82, typed/native hash22 and staged input51, including a same-allocation shipping-WASM decode. ## Historical locally accepted runtime-startup snapshot

W04 coherent initialization is implemented in [runtime bootstrap](src/build/runtime.ts), [strict verification](src/build/verification.ts), [native loading](src/build/native.ts), [core client](src/document/coreClient.ts), [document worker](src/workers/document.worker.ts), [camera](src/input/camera.ts) and [main entry](src/main.tsx). The controller awaits compatible scene-free core readiness before graphics/camera readiness and transfers that startup client into the first load candidate. Production compares compiled requirements with `build-manifest.json`, verifies role/URL closure and bounded bytes, then executes those worker/glue/WASM bytes. The tab pins its accepted set. Development reports `development-module-graph`; it checks native identities/readiness without claiming the production module closure.

[Build generation](tools/web-build.mjs) prepares `src/generated/buildIdentity.ts`, checks source freshness, emits self-contained application/worker bundles and writes the final manifest after HTML SRI and all output files exist. Native static getters report ABI6/core and ABI1/camera, scalar variant, canonical input ID and semantic revisions before scene/input allocation. The [frozen runtime-startup validation record](docs/validation/runtime-handshake-local.json) binds native input ID `65cab916c99c63585cdd969167db78c7b3f281bbb7361af0d1ac7e54ad24a210`; current corrected-wrapper provenance has the newer identity recorded above.

The bounded verified-byte startup foundation is **locally accepted** on the [last accepted frozen validation record](docs/validation/runtime-handshake-local.json): **305/305 units across eight files, 470/470 full development-browser cases across seventeen files and 47/47 production-preview cases**, followed by a fresh production build. The full run used two workers and completed in 579.6 seconds (9.7 minutes); production preview took 31.6 seconds with one worker. Both have zero failures, skips or flaky cases. Twenty native-verifier and nineteen web-build checks, clean native compilation, real static-identity probes and the artifact audit also pass. This accepts the recorded startup boundaries. That frozen packet did not execute the genuine semantic A/B and UI-only C browser matrix; the newer seventeen passing coherent03 cells above cover its bounded local cases. Complete W04 qualification still requires held-client failures, successful-winner late events, cleanup-admission, actual host/offline updates, matching Windows execution and actual Mac performance.

The first [new full development regression](test-results-runtime-handshake-all-01.json) finished with **469 passes and one failed Background Color-owner/retained-map case** in 585.5 seconds, with no skips or flaky results. The test-only harness rendered with default view arguments during pending raw-map work; its four calls now use the production grade/highlight/identity-geometry/selected-scope arguments, preserving intentional tone/empty states, guards and oracles. Runtime, native and deployed artifact bytes are unchanged. The corrected [Background focus](test-results-runtime-handshake-background-02.json) passes **33/33 cases (eleven repeated three times) in 14.0 seconds**. Preserve full attempt 01 as a diagnostic; its counts are not combined with later runs.

The [211-input source capture](docs/validation/runtime-handshake-local-inputs-02.json), canonical file-list SHA-256 `7c65d196b13d930bcb6773d640918399dc858a4ca7d4b8123f15c9d05ac20d4d`, remained unchanged through full attempt02 and the [fresh post-run build](test-results-runtime-handshake-build-final-01.log). The [artifact audit](test-results-runtime-handshake-artifact-audit-02.json) verifies the same 92-artifact deployment, SRI and native copies. ## Preserved cleanup qualification

The locally accepted W04 packet extends [App](src/ui/App.tsx), [controller](src/app/controller.ts), [selection session](src/input/selectionSession.ts) and [store](src/app/store.ts) with retained terminal-cleanup authority. Its exact session/client/document owner survives failed or unknown restoration; `idle`, `restoring` and `required` state drives Restore selection and operation interlocks. One concurrent restoration attempt joins explicit `retrySelectionCleanup()` requests without an automatic loop. Historical cleanup qualification passed **272 units, 141 focused browser cases and 470 full browser cases**, followed by a production build; the frozen accepted runtime-startup snapshot repeats the full retained 470-case browser inventory with 305 units and a fresh build. The newer corrected-wrapper packet still needs full regression. This accepts the bounded local cleanup path; general lost-acknowledgement/terminated-worker recovery and Mac/performance qualification remain open.

Only explicit `true` begin/preview/confirm/cancel acknowledgements count as accepted. Unknown begin closes its attempt without automatically resubmitting; successful confirm retires the draft, while false, thrown or unknown restoration retains authority. Failed finish preserves unresolved `cleanupNeeded`, and duplicate finish calls join rather than starting another outcome. Mutations, history, new gestures, Color/Fill, maps, capture, export and replacement remain blocked while cleanup is unresolved. The historical units and real controller/UI/native/GPU failure cases cover these bounded contracts; the new 305-unit run repeats the unit portion. Successful ordinary restoration preserves an ongoing live-Single press and its original Combine; unresolved `required` cleanup ends that drag. Native algorithm-worker failure remains separate.

## Local evidence

The [last accepted runtime-startup record](docs/validation/runtime-handshake-local.json) establishes bounded local acceptance from 305 units, 470 development-browser cases, 47 production-preview cases and a fresh post-regression build on the unchanged 211-input capture. The tables above identify terminal receipts and remaining scope. The following cleanup/gesture records are historical and are not relabeled as current source.

The historical cleanup source passed **272/272 units across seven files**, including 175 real-core and 28 selection-session cases, **141/141 focused browser cases across four files** and **470/470 full browser cases across 17 files**. Both browser runs used two workers and had no failures, skips or flaky cases; their durations were 3.4 and 12.5 minutes. Fresh production compilation passed after the terminal full run. Neither the previous gesture packet nor the historical cleanup packet changed or rebuilt native code. The new handshake packet does rebuild both modules with static identities. The earlier clean 84-target build evidence is historical; artifacts passed provenance verification before legacy-fixture capture and through the cleanup production freshness gate, alongside the earlier fifteen verifier probes. The GPU sort fixture checks every ID in 65,777 entries; rendering/edit/export fixtures are small analytic cases, not representative scene-size benchmarks or actual-Mac qualification.

Frozen-source review exposed viewer-wrapper distinctions that the earlier browser tests did not model correctly. W17e.6 now corrects the implementation and replaces those expectations with independent baseline, owner, preference and history checks, including the initial valid-contributor/transparent-map case and cancellation races. Passing direct selection-kernel tests alone does not establish the desktop viewer wrapper; a matching Windows executable and paired browser comparisons remain required; dedicated Mac comparisons are optional under the active scope.

The historical coherent03 ABI6 core WASM is 1,447,146 bytes with SHA-256 `160917584fbb670893edaf69a2c8510abb3533eafa747dd7916b92eb646752a9`. Later live-preview build02 core is 1,461,361 bytes, SHA-256 `ca1855e2c980544642c6b13cf202507959ed622e4069f1e095577fbbd1de9b82`; [probe04](test-results-live-preview-native-probe-04.json) binds exact qualified artifacts/current IDs. Units/focus/production/final build and source/artifact consistency pass; terminal full04 failures leave complete current-source acceptance pending. Shipping Fill remains four-neighbor; matching Windows/Mac/host/offline qualification is open.

The Chromium runner currently uses `--enable-unsafe-webgpu`. Its result verifies this local development configuration, not default-browser GPU availability. Record shipping-browser versions, adapters and normal launch behavior during qualification.

Graphics recovery rebuilds resources from the same live authoritative worker. Locally accepted W04 reconciliation releases required cleanup ownership only after exact client/revision agreement, healthy loaded native state, no transaction/draft/pending operation, a null pending map and successful replacement GPU publication. Failure keeps the required owner; reconciliation does not auto-commit a checkpoint or clear general `editingFailed`. Real browser cases cover failed replacement publication, native-checkpoint refusal and coherent recovery, including a store observer that sees ready only after the recovery lock clears. This bounded path does not reconstruct a terminated worker or resolve a lost committed-edit acknowledgement.

Volume/visible Brush borrow immutable map channels, but sampling, source/spatial work and candidate publication remain synchronous WASM. Cancellation cannot interrupt a running native call. The locally accepted W04 path retains the exact cleanup owner after refused terminal restoration, even if admission later recovers; boolean cancellation and explicit Restore selection retry keep conflicting operations blocked until an acknowledged outcome. Current units and real controller/UI/native/GPU [failure cases](WEB_PORT_PLAN.md#decisions-to-close-before-broader-acceptance) pass within the full/build-qualified local packet. Broader unknown committed-edit reconciliation and terminated-worker recovery remain open. Tracked allocators cover consumer buffers, while complete simultaneous native/JS/GPU peaks remain unqualified. The [consumer contract](WEB_IMPLEMENTATION_DETAILS.md#implemented-volume-and-visible-brush-consumer-contract) records those boundaries.

Fill similarly borrows retained immutable channels and synchronously evaluates its flood/source footprint. Its admitted region mask is row-padded: `8 * ceil(mapWidth/64) * mapHeight` bytes, in addition to visited/queue/checkpoint/COW/history work. A thin map can cost substantially more than `P/8` region bytes. Browser abort prevents stale publication and waits for rollback/retirement; it cannot interrupt the running native kernel. The [Fill contract](WEB_IMPLEMENTATION_DETAILS.md#implemented-fill-and-map-checkpoint-contract) separates local correctness from complete peak and hardware qualification.

Connected Color reuses the flood/retention path, adds native touched-hit and SH/Combine work, and retains its map while toggled off. The [implemented contract](WEB_IMPLEMENTATION_DETAILS.md#implemented-connected-color-contract) now distinguishes requested preference, logical waiting, acquired map availability and accepted native owner/map metadata. W17e.6 has locally verified the following frozen viewer-wrapper outcomes:

| Boundary | Implemented local result | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cancel a waiting first RGB upgrade | Keep committed mask/history and requested Connected preference; discard the waiting adjustable owner. Later tolerance cannot amend it. Off clears waiting, and an acquired map remains nonwaiting through publication. | Held real readback, off/on, newer tolerance during publication, exact work-lease retirement and rejection of late results |
| Empty first Connected amendment | Restore the original pre-click baseline; retain the original owner/map and requested Connected mode. Returning to baseline can remove the originating Undo item; off recomputes ordinary Color from that checkpoint. | All Combine modes, nonempty baselines, exact exports, repeated empty amendments, no-op/Redo and actual refusal rollback as a separate outcome |
| Initial valid-contributor Connected click with a transparent mapped seed | Replace the previous adjustable owner with a new Connected no-op owner/map using the current pre-click baseline. Preserve mask/history/Redo; off evaluates the new contributor/operation. Missing-contributor Color and empty Fill still preserve the prior owner. | Independent native all-Combine replacement fixtures and real controller/GPU/export/map lifetime assertions |
| Queue-empty Basic/Pro change during the amendment | Allow the profile transition while retaining the same owner/query and captured operation/options. Explicit option assignment still ends ownership. | Both real App directions with distinct remembered profiles and explicit assignment of an unchanged option |
| Older cancellation joins after a new gesture starts | Cancel only the geometric owner captured at cancellation entry; never capture a newer gesture after awaiting old tolerance cleanup. | Deterministic held-query cancellation followed by a new Brush gesture |

The source authority is the frozen [viewer tolerance/cancellation wrapper](reference/windows/src/viewer_selection.cpp), [selection kernel](reference/windows/src/selection.cpp), [history adjustment](reference/windows/src/editing.cpp) and [profile transition](reference/windows/src/viewer.cpp). The [W17e.6 record](WEB_IMPLEMENTATION_DETAILS.md#connected-viewer-fidelity-corrections-w17e6) separates source traces from the terminal local core/browser receipts. Native executable, actual Mac, dense latency and complete aggregate peak qualification remain open.

W16q now implements FIFO released clicks and deferred Basic/Pro changes. Records retain captured view/options and immutable release revision; explicitly owned commit/rollback results advance compatible expected revisions. They reserve estimated 1 KiB bookkeeping each, with a maximum of 1,024 waiting/executing records. That estimate is not actual JS/process heap evidence. An executing record remains counted through amendments and terminal cleanup, and default cancellation snapshots only existing records. New compatible input survives older cancellation.

Live-tail setters update only their explicit field. Empty tails do not transfer preferences to a previous owner; after draining, tolerance preserves the surviving owner's mode even if the global Connected preference differs. An actual Connected change adopts global Color tolerance, while assigning the same requested preference is a no-op. Canceled bookkeeping cannot intercept setters; explicit changes during a canceled candidate publication apply to the owner that actually survives. Save joins admitted work and final amendments; external commands/navigation fence new admission. The [boundary acceptance record](WEB_IMPLEMENTATION_DETAILS.md#w16q-engineering-steps-and-invariants) now includes passing saturation, initial-dispatch setter, dirty/immediate-navigation and keyboard-Save traces. Hardware measurements, graphics-retry admission and future folder navigation remain separate work.

The [feature delivery ledger](docs/parity/feature-matrix.csv) records 33 capabilities and their remaining release evidence. WebGL viewing is a separate compatibility deliverable. Native integrations are explicitly excluded; unfinished feasible editor features remain in the full WebGPU release scope.

| Recovery/host | Resolve unknown edit acknowledgement/reconstruction, complete changed-boundary initialization/failure cases, then actual HTTPS/MIME/cache/offline/update and repeated lifecycle checks. Existing local receipts do not accept these gates. |

The configured development caps remain one million loaded preview splats, 256 MiB input, a 1 GiB aggregate reservation ledger and 512 MiB native resource budget. Central checks across controller, probe, client and worker, including intrinsic Blob-size validation, have focused local qualification above. Future unpacked/grouped input uses the same 256 MiB aggregate-original scene cap plus per-resource limits. All caps remain subject to decoded format/device checks and operation admission. They do not establish a physical-memory ceiling, guaranteed ten-step geometry history or universal 30 fps.

## Verification commands

Use Node 24 or later, Python, CMake/Ninja and the pinned Emscripten 6.0.12 SDK. `EMSDK` can point to an activated installation; the local build also recognizes `.tools/emsdk`. Frozen source inputs are already captured; do not recapture over them.

```text
npm ci
npm run build:core
python tools/verify-toolchain.py verify-build --local-evidence
python -m unittest discover -s tools -p test_verify_toolchain.py
npm run prepare:web
npm run test:web-build
npm test
npm run typecheck:bench
npm run test:bench
npx playwright install chromium
npm run test:e2e
npm run build
npm run dev
```

`build:core` verifies the frozen inputs, installed tool/runtime/cache fingerprints and pinned codec archives/trees; removes ambient build overrides, stale reports and root CMake state; recreates the adapter overlay; and compiles with `--clean-first`. It rejects changed inputs during compilation and records command/input/output hashes in `public/wasm/build-provenance.json`. `verify-build --local-evidence` checks report freshness and local command/configuration evidence. `build` verifies sources/product data and native provenance, generates the application input descriptor, typechecks, bundles self-contained application/worker output, rewrites entry/style SRI, then writes the final deployment manifest and rechecks inputs/copied native bytes. Runtime bootstrap independently verifies the compiled descriptor, exact roles/URLs/bytes and actual native identities before editor readiness. Final deployment verification must additionally run from the production output and the real hosting URL; a passing development server does not prove cache updates, MIME types, offline use or browser permissions.

### Opt-in SteadySplats (October 25, 2026)

Implemented session-only Gaussian renderer settings, stochastic unsorted
visibility, four-neighbor spatial resampling, temporal reconstruction and
16-frame default convergence. The existing controller retains render demand,
memory admission and recovery ownership. Sorted rendering is the default and
fallback, and remains authoritative for exact picking/maps and clean captures.

Current portable checks passed 6,402 tests. The initial development cohort passed
51 real-browser checks, including SteadySplats, Settings, Basic workflows,
geometry, histogram and product policy, with no retries. Browser evidence is
Windows Chromium 156 / nonfallback NVIDIA Blackwell, secure localhost without
cross-origin isolation or optional WebGPU features. The [feature review](design/feature-reviews/steady-splats-2026-10-25.md)
links final build/production results and retained diagnostics. Other browsers,
native editors and preview providers require their own qualification.

### WebGL2 implementation checkpoint (October 25, 2026)

Forced WebGL2 now renders sorted Gaussians/PLY and opt-in SteadySplats using the
reviewed shared frame state. Exact worker picks/maps, clean image readback,
display histogram and context-loss canvas replacement are implemented and have
focused observed-browser evidence. The
[active review](design/feature-reviews/webgl2-implementation-2026-10-25.md)
records checks and remaining qualification. Default startup still uses WebGPU;
automatic fallback, the remaining full-editor operating envelope and final
production qualification are pending. Earlier WebGPU receipts remain historical.

### Rendering throughput checkpoint (October 25, 2026)

The controller admits up to two matching display submissions while retaining
serialized CPU preparation and genuine GPU completion/retirement. Moving sorted
views use 24-bit keys and refine to exact 32-bit order at rest on both backends.
WebGPU optionally compacts the sorted draw list at 262,144 sources and above;
resource refusal retains portable full drawing. Exact queries/capture remain
full precision. Standalone defaults remain 32-bit, with typed precision and
optional host admission/motion helpers.

The [review](design/feature-reviews/rendering-throughput-2026-10-25.md) and
[delivery receipt](docs/validation/runs/rendering-throughput-20261025-01/validation.json)
record this packet's source identities, actual checks, quality, before/after
measurements and rejected candidates. External-engine performance parity and
other browser/OS/GPU qualification remain unproven.
